A scoring machine 
Perhaps the finest offensive performance by an individual came in a 1990 1A semifinal. Mardela’s Tia Jackson scored 44 of her team’s 52 points.   
Most amazingly, Tia made only a single trip to the free throw line. She accomplished her feat with eleven 2 pointers, seven 3 pointers, and was 1 for 2 from the charity stripe.

- In 1976, Loch Raven’s Janet Flora  posted three individual 
tournament scoring records, two of which still stand.  - Sandy and Curtis Graff  are the first married couple to each 
have won a state championship.  Sandy led Smithsburg to the 1981 Girls Class C title.  Curtis coached North Hager-stown to the Girls Class A title in 1985.     - Stan Kernan of Parkdale  is the only coach to win both a 
boys state title (1971 AA, 1976 AA) and a girls state title (1981 AA).  - Glenn and Alice Smith  are the first father-daughter 
coaches to each have won a state championship.  Glenn led the Williamsport girls to the 1992 Class 1A title.  Five years later, his daughter Alice coached the Francis Scott Key girls to the 1997 Class 1A title.  
7MILDRED HANEY MURRAY  
SPORTSMANSHIP AWARD  
     The Maryland Public Secondary Schools Athletic Association  annually presents a sportsmanship trophy at the Girls State Ba s-
ketball Tournament.  The Mildred Haney Murray Sportsmanship Award will remain a permanent possession of the school selected.

20JACK WILLARD SPORTSMANSHIP AWARD  
 
     The Maryland Public Secondary Schools Athletic Association  annually presents a sportsmanship trophy at the Boys’ State 
Basketball Tournament.  The Jack Willard Sportsmanship Award  will remain a permanent possession of the school selected.

78DOUG SUTHERLAND SPORTSMANSHIP AWARD  
     The Maryland Public Secondary Schools Athletic Association began presenting the Doug Sutherland 
Sportsmanship Awards at the State Championship meets in 2017.  Doug Sutherland was a member of the original State Swimming Committee representing Prince George’s County and was well-known as a passionate supporter of the sport.       The awards are presented to the school in each of the two classifications that has displayed the best overall sportsmanship (girls and boys) during the State Meets as voted on by participating coaches and officials.  The Sportsmanship Awards are then presented to the winning schools during the scheduled awards ceremony for each classification at the State Championship Meets.

70 Tyrone Neal, Southern-AA 71 Tyrone Neal, Southern-AA 72 Jim Seymour, Bladensburg 73 Joe Wojocowski, Hereford Individual Champions 1970-2025
NOTE : Prior to the 14 newly adjusted weight classes in 2011-12, the last wholesale shift in weight classes occurred in 
1988, when the lowest weight class was increased from 98 to 103 pounds. Other changes since 1988 were in 2002, when the number of classes went from 13 to 14 and the 215-pound weight class became mandatory, and in 2006 when the 275-pound class was increased to 285 pounds. In 2024, there was a shift down beginning with the 145-pound weight class and ending with the 220-pound class.

Maryland State 
WRESTLING HALL OF FAME   
The MARYLAND STATE WRESTLING HALL OF FAME  was established in 1975 to honor 
those persons who have dedicated so much of their time to the sport of high school 
wrestling within the State or to outstanding coaches or wrestlers who have given promi-
nence to the sport of wrestling through their achievements.  The following are so honored:

101SPORTSMANSHIP AWARD
The Mar
yland Public Secondary Schools Athletic Association presents a sportsmanship award annually 
at the state wrestling tournament, a tradition established in 2016. The trophy is presented to the one 
team displaying the best overall sportsmanship during the Dual Meet Wrestling Championships. 
Coaches, players, cheerleaders, and spectators from each participating school are evaluated. Raters in-
clude state committee members, region directors, officials, and table personnel.  
Previous Winners  
2016 Oakland Mills 
2017 Leonardtown 2018 North Caroline 2019 Bel Air 2020 Manchester Valley 2021
Season cancelled due to COVID-19 pandemic.
